**Concept category:** carbocation stability
**Source:** Using the Research Literature to Develop an Adaptive Intervention to Improve Student Explanations of an S_N1 Reaction Mechanism (using-the-research-literature-to-develop-an-adaptive-intervention-to-improve-student-explanations-of-an-sn1-reaction/using-the-research-literature-to-develop-an-adaptive-intervention-to-improve-student-explanations-of-an-sn1-reaction.md)
**Evidence:** "students commonly invoked a 'number of substituents' argument when comparing mechanistic steps involving the formation of a carbocation."

## Misconception 444: Students focus only on explicit structural features and do not infer electron density when judging carbocation stability.

**Topic:** Carbocation stability / Electron density
**Correct concept:** Carbocation stability should be linked to inferred electron density and electron-donating effects, not only visible structural counts.
**Expected reasoning:** A student should infer how surrounding groups affect the distribution of electron density around the cation and use that to explain why certain carbocations are more stable than others.
**Concept category:** carbocation stability
**Source:** Using the Research Literature to Develop an Adaptive Intervention to Improve Student Explanations of an S_N1 Reaction Mechanism (using-the-research-literature-to-develop-an-adaptive-intervention-to-improve-student-explanations-of-an-sn1-reaction/using-the-research-literature-to-develop-an-adaptive-intervention-to-improve-student-explanations-of-an-sn1-reaction.md)
**Evidence:** "students who referred to implicit properties of the reaction in their explanations were more successful than those who only referred to explicit properties"

## Misconception 445: Students identify nucleophiles and electrophiles mainly by charge or obvious structural features, rather than by electron density.

**Topic:** Nucleophilic addition / Identifying nucleophiles and electrophiles
**Correct concept:** Nucleophiles are electron-rich species and electrophiles are electron-poor species; charge alone is not sufficient.
**Expected reasoning:** A student should look for areas of high electron density to identify nucleophiles and areas of low electron density to identify electrophiles, then predict attraction between them.
**Concept category:** nucleophile/electrophile identification
**Source:** Using the Research Literature to Develop an Adaptive Intervention to Improve Student Explanations of an S_N1 Reaction Mechanism (using-the-research-literature-to-develop-an-adaptive-intervention-to-improve-student-explanations-of-an-sn1-reaction/using-the-research-literature-to-develop-an-adaptive-intervention-to-improve-student-explanations-of-an-sn1-reaction.md)
**Evidence:** "students relied heavily on charge and other structural features (such as lone pairs) when identifying nucleophiles and electrophiles."

## Misconception 446: Students think an electrophile is simply anything with a positive charge.

**Topic:** Nucleophilic addition / Electrophile definition
**Correct concept:** An electrophile is an electron-poor species that accepts an electron pair; positive charge may be associated with electrophilicity but is not the whole definition.
**Expected reasoning:** A student should determine whether a site has low electron density and can accept electron density from a nucleophile, rather than using charge alone as the criterion.
**Concept category:** nucleophile/electrophile identification
**Source:** Using the Research Literature to Develop an Adaptive Intervention to Improve Student Explanations of an S_N1 Reaction Mechanism (using-the-research-literature-to-develop-an-adaptive-intervention-to-improve-student-explanations-of-an-sn1-reaction/using-the-research-literature-to-develop-an-adaptive-intervention-to-improve-student-explanations-of-an-sn1-reaction.md)
**Evidence:** "an electrophile is just something with a positive charge"

## Misconception 447: Students may identify a nucleophile without identifying an electrophile, suggesting they do not see electrophiles as equally important in reaction reasoning.

**Topic:** Nucleophilic addition / Nucleophile/electrophile recognition
**Correct concept:** Mechanistic reasoning requires identifying both the electron-rich nucleophile and the electron-poor electrophile because reaction occurs through their interaction.
**Expected reasoning:** A student should locate both reacting partners and explain how electron density moves from the nucleophile to the electrophile, since both are needed for the bond-forming step.
**Concept category:** nucleophile/electrophile identification
**Source:** Using the Research Literature to Develop an Adaptive Intervention to Improve Student Explanations of an S_N1 Reaction Mechanism (using-the-research-literature-to-develop-an-adaptive-intervention-to-improve-student-explanations-of-an-sn1-reaction/using-the-research-literature-to-develop-an-adaptive-intervention-to-improve-student-explanations-of-an-sn1-reaction.md)
**Evidence:** "many students identified a nucleophile without identifying an electrophile"

## Misconception 448: Students conflate basicity and nucleophilicity or do not distinguish between them.

**Topic:** Acid-base proton-transfer / Basicity vs nucleophilicity
**Correct concept:** Basicity and nucleophilicity are distinct concepts, though a species can act as both in the same mechanism depending on the context.
**Expected reasoning:** A student should decide whether a species is donating an electron pair to a proton (base) or to a nonproton electrophile (nucleophile) and distinguish the two roles in the mechanism.
**Concept category:** acid-base concepts
**Source:** Using the Research Literature to Develop an Adaptive Intervention to Improve Student Explanations of an S_N1 Reaction Mechanism (using-the-research-literature-to-develop-an-adaptive-intervention-to-improve-student-explanations-of-an-sn1-reaction/using-the-research-literature-to-develop-an-adaptive-intervention-to-improve-student-explanations-of-an-sn1-reaction.md)
**Evidence:** "students' challenges with confusing basicity and nucleophilicity"

## Misconception 449: Students can correctly use Brønsted-Lowry ideas but struggle to apply the Lewis acid-base model in organic mechanism explanations.

**Topic:** Acid-base proton-transfer / Lewis acid-base reasoning
**Correct concept:** Lewis acid-base reasoning uses electron pairs: bases donate an electron pair and acids accept an electron pair.
**Expected reasoning:** A student should track electron pairs, identify the electron-rich donor and electron-poor acceptor, and use that to explain proton transfer mechanistically.
**Concept category:** acid-base concepts
**Prevalence:** fewer than half could correctly use the Lewis model
**Population:** undergraduate students
**Source:** Using the Research Literature to Develop an Adaptive Intervention to Improve Student Explanations of an S_N1 Reaction Mechanism (using-the-research-literature-to-develop-an-adaptive-intervention-to-improve-student-explanations-of-an-sn1-reaction/using-the-research-literature-to-develop-an-adaptive-intervention-to-improve-student-explanations-of-an-sn1-reaction.md)
**Evidence:** "most undergraduate students could correctly use the Brønsted-Lowry model, but fewer than half could correctly use the Lewis model."
